Which model best describes the mature enzyme, including how its subunits arise?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> A homodimer of two identical subunits, produced by autocatalytic cleavage.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> A single, uncleaved polypeptide that functions as a monomer.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> A heterodimer of alpha and beta subunits, produced by cleavage by a separate protease.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> A heterodimer of alpha and beta subunits, produced by autocatalytic cleavage.<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> GO_molecular_function_0142:1<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Step 1: Analyze the protein sequence architecture**<br>- **Signal peptide**: The N-terminal hydrophobic stretch &quot;MGTPAIRAACHGAHLALALLLLLSLSD&quot; is clearly a signal peptide, indicating a secreted protein.<br>- **Propeptide region**: Following the signal peptide, there is a propeptide segment that will be removed during maturation.<br>- **Functional domain before catalytic domain**: The region containing what appears to be a kringle-like domain (with characteristic cysteine pattern and fold) precedes the catalytic domain.<br>- **Catalytic domain**: The motif &quot;IADVYYIVGGTSPQ&quot; is a hallmark of the chymotrypsin-like serine protease catalytic domain, where the &quot;IVGG&quot; motif marks the N-terminus of the mature catalytic subunit.<br><br>**Step 2: Biological context**<br>The overall domain architecture — signal peptide + propeptide + N-terminal functional domain (likely kringle) + serine protease catalytic domain — is characteristic of the plasminogen activator family (tPA, uPA) or coagulation cascade serine proteases. These proteins are secreted as single-chain zymogens (precursors).<br><br>**Step 3: Subunit organization**<br>In these proteins, the single-chain precursor is proteolytically cleaved at a specific Arg/Lys-Ile bond (just before the IVGG motif), generating two chains connected by disulfide bond(s) — forming a heterodimer.<br><br>**Step 4: Mechanism of cleavage**<br>For all well-characterized members of this family (tPA, uPA, coagulation factors IX, X, II):<br>- The activation cleavage is performed by a **separate protease** (plasmin for tPA/uPA; factor XIa, VIIa/tissue factor, or prothrombinase complex for coagulation factors).<br>- This is NOT autocatalytic cleavage.<br><br>**Step 5: Evaluate each option**<br>- **A** (homodimer, autocatalytic): Incorrect — the two chains are different (non-identical), ruling out homodimer.<br>- **B** (monomer): Incorrect — the presence of distinct domains separated by an activation cleavage site indicates a heterodimeric mature form.<br>- **C** (heterodimer, separate protease): Correct — matches the established activation mechanism for this class of proteins.<br>- **D** (heterodimer, autocatalytic): Incorrect — while the heterodimeric structure is correct, the cleavage is by a separate protease, not autocatalytic.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
